Why is Pin Bhaba a Must-Do Trek?
The Pin Bhaba Pass trek stands among those very rare journeys that offer an extraordinary transition of scenery, culture, and terrain. This thrilling high-altitude (16,100 ft) adventure perfectly blends both beauty and challenge.
This trek is known for the most spectacular landscape contrast. Here, you will witness one of the sharpest transitions in the Himalayan beauty.
The Pin Bhaba Pass trek is a rare combination of Kinnaur and Spiti regions, thus connecting two culturally and geographically distinct worlds.
On this mesmerizing trek, you’ll experience changing colors, textures, and terrain almost every day. This is probably the nicest way to enjoy the best of both worlds in a single journey.
Crossing the Pin Bhaba Pass is as much rewarding as thrilling. The climb is exciting and physically challenging, while the steep ascents add to the adventure.
The mountain peaks and landscapes visible from the pass are absolutely incredible. It offers snow-covered ridges, rugged peaks of the Spiti mountain range, glaciated slopes, and alpine meadows on the Kinnaur side.
The trek is known for some of the most breathtaking high-altitude campsites in the Himalayas. For example, camping beside glacial streams at Mulling, watching sunset at Phutsirang, and experiencing starry skies at Kara.
The glimpses of rich Himalayan biodiversity on this trek are really delightful. Pine forests, wildflowers, Himalayan birds, and mammals like blue sheep and red foxes make it visually dynamic.
River crossings, glacier walks, and rocky trails create a true expedition feel. The terrain constantly changes, keeping the journey exciting.
This journey is one of the best ways to experience the Buddhist culture of Spiti and the ancient traditions of Kinnaur. You can visit ancient monasteries in Kaza after the trek.
Major Attractions of the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek
The Surprising Landscape Transition
The most prominent attraction of the Pin Bhaba Pass trek is that it feels like different worlds are stitched together into one journey. You start in the lush green and vibrant valleys on one side and end in the cold desert on the other. As you begin the trek, you walk through dense pine and oak forests, green meadows, waterfalls, and mountain streams. After crossing the pass, you suddenly find yourself in the midst of brown, barren mountains, rocks and boulders, and cold desert.

Forest Trails Along the Bhaba River
The first few days of trekking are extremely scenic, and you continuously walk beside the Bhaba River. During this enchanting journey, you come across dense pine forests where trees seem to have been standing timelessly. The wooden bridges reflect how people of this region use their traditional skills to sustain. The sound of the river stays with you almost throughout the early part of the trek. The monsoon months feel magical here.

Breathtaking Mulling Meadows
Mulling is probably the prettiest campsite, which is constantly pulsating with greenery, and the Bhaba River cuts right through the valley. The rolling green slopes of this place are extremely picturesque, while clouds seem to be floating at arm’s length. The snow-clad mountains in the backdrop of wide green grasslands appear like freshly painted. The display of wildflowers during monsoons throughout the valley is stunning. Since there is no light pollution, night skies seem incredible all the time.

Phutsirang Glacier Region
As you trek higher, the area around Phutsirang becomes more rugged and dramatic. This stunning region is no less than a high-altitude wonderland, where lush meadows unite with striking glaciers. The highlights of this region are marvellous glacier views, gleaming snow patches, and high-altitude moraine sections. This is where the trek starts feeling truly adventurous and remote. The greenery slowly disappears as you approach the pass.

Crossing Pin Bhaba Pass
Crossing the Pass at an altitude of 16,100 feet is not just an emotional moment but also a stunning visual climax of the trek. The views from the pass are absolutely dramatic. From the top, one side of the trek shows the lush green Bhaba Valley, while the other side reveals barren Spiti landscapes. This contrast is what makes the adventure truly legendary among the trekkers. The climb to the pass includes snowfields, loose scree, steep ascents, and strong winds. Besides, the panoramic views from the top are unforgettable.

Mudh Village in Pin Valley
Perched at 3,800 meters (12,500 ft), Mudh Village is a serene heaven of untouched beauty that serves as the gateway to the Pin Valley. This place marks the end of the legendary Pin Bhaba Pass trek and offers you a completely arid and colorful desert landscape. After reaching here, you’ll witness a rewarding sight – traditional Tibetan-style homes, Tibetan Buddhist culture, vibrant green fields, and a dramatic panorama of towering mountains.

Adventure in Stream Crossings
One of the most exciting and thrilling parts of the trek is crossing multiple gushing, cold streams. These fast-flowing mountain streams make your trek exciting if you are looking for something adventurous. More importantly, coming together as a team and helping each other feels absolutely fascinating. Other adventures you encounter on this trek are boulder sections, snow crossings, long trekking hours, and sudden weather changes.

Cultural Contrast Between Kinnaur and Spiti
One of the key highlights of this trek is the distinct cultural and geographical identities of the Kinnaur and Spiti regions. The cultural transition is as stark as the landscape transition, if not more. You’ll observe that the Kinnaur side has Hindu influence, green agricultural villages, and wood-based architecture. The Spiti side, on the other hand, has more pronounced Tibetan Buddhist culture, flat-roofed mud houses, monasteries and prayer flags.

Pin Valley National Park
Pin Valley National Park is one of the highlights of this trek due to its stark Himalayan beauty, rich biodiversity, and unique rugged landscape. What makes this place especially memorable is after crossing lush green valleys, you enter a barren yet striking terrain of jagged mountains, deep gorges, and wide-open landscapes. This place is also known as a high-altitude biodiversity hotspot. It is home to rare wildlife such as the elusive snow leopard, Himalayan wolf, Himalayan blue sheep (Bharal), red fox, and Himalayan marmot.

Best Time to Do the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek
The best time to do the Pin Bhaba Pass trek is during the monsoon months from July to September, with each month infusing a unique character.

Unlike other Himalayan treks, where the monsoon plays a spoilsport, Pin Bhaba Pass comes alive and becomes spectacular during this period.

The lush green meadows of Bhaba valley and the cold desert of the Pin valley offer two dramatically contrasting ecosystems. The monsoon months are when this contrast is at its peak.

Here is the breakdown in terms of each monsoon month:

July
The month of July is mainly known for the wildflower explosion. The route comes alive after the snow melts. You will witness: bright green meadows, overflowing streams, vibrant alpine flowers, and fresh snow patches near the pass.

The daytime temperature ranges from 5 to 15 degrees; nighttime temperature drops to 0 to -5 degrees.

August
The month of August is considered as the most balanced and popular month. Many trekkers call it the sweet spot for the Pin Bhaba Pass. You will see: stabilized trails, flowers still blooming around, snow mostly melted, and temperatures becoming more pleasant and comfortable.

The unique attribute of this month is the nice balance of scenery + accessibility.In August, the average daytime temperature is 12 to 20 degrees, and at night, it drops to 5 to -5 degrees or slightly below.

September
September is calmer, clearer, and more dramatic in a different way. It is also known as the golden transition month. You will see: the monsoon beginning retreating, the landscapes transforming from lush greenery to golden grasslands, clear skies, and sharper mountain views. Thus, in this month, you essentially witness epic Himalayan vastness with dramatic skies and golden high-altitude terrain.

In September, the average daytime temperature ranges from 15 to 18 degrees, while nights drop to 5 to -1 degrees.

How Difficult is the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek
The Pin Bhaba Pass trek is considered a moderate-to-difficult journey. Covering an approximate distance of 51 km and reaching a maximum altitude of around 16,100 feet, this trek requires good physical fitness, prior high-altitude trekking experience, and high mental endurance to tackle the difficult transitions of the terrain.

Here are the challenges the Pin Bhaba Pass trek offers:

High Altitude and Steep Climbs: In a matter of just a few days, you have to go through a substantial altitude gain. That is, from 8,000 ft to 16,100 ft, the elevation change is massive. Moreover, the summit push is steep and requires trekking on loose scree, boulders, and snow.
Abrupt Terrain Changes: It is one of those few treks where the terrain sees a considerable transition. One day, you are walking through the green meadows, and the other day, you encounter rocky trails full of boulders and moraines on steep climbs. From the lush, marshy valleys of the Bhaba region to the stark, rocky terrain of Spiti, the trail constantly shifts through dramatically diverse landscapes.
Demanding River Crossings: The trek has several glacier-fed streams, and some of them are fast-flowing without any bridges. This makes them very challenging to cross. These sharp and freezing waters can pose a real challenge for first-time trekkers.
Harsh Environments: The altitude of Pin Bhaba Pass is well within the risk of acute mountain sickness due to massive elevation gain and unpredictable sub-zero temperatures.
How Pin Bhaba Got Its Name
Pin Bhaba Pass derives its name from the two valleys it links — the rugged Pin Valley of Spiti, known for its stark cold-desert terrain and Tibetan Buddhist heritage, and the thriving Bhaba Valley of Kinnaur, filled with forests, rivers, and alpine villages. The pass essentially serves as a natural bridge between these two contrasting Himalayan worlds.

Traditionally and historically, this trek has served as a popular Himalayan corridor. Local people from Spiti and Kinnaur used this route for seasonal migration, shepherding, barter trade, and movement between valleys.

Merchants, monks, shepherds, and pilgrims all moved through such routes depending on the season. That is, for local mountain communities, it was extremely important.

How to Get Fit for the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek
Before going on a moderate trek to a difficult trek such as Pin Bhaba Pass, it’s crucial to follow a comprehensive fitness regimen for ideally 3 - 4 months beforehand. Your workout should be a balanced mix of cardio and strength.

Focus on improving cardiovascular endurance through activities like brisk walking, jogging, cycling, or stair climbing. For strength training, you should do exercises such as squats, lunges, planks, and push-ups. This will help you build stamina and stability.

You can also practice hiking with a loaded backpack to prepare your body for real trekking conditions. Finally, flexibility and balance exercises, including yoga and stretching, reduce the risk of injuries and muscle cramps.

For more information regarding fitness preparation, click this link and download our trek exercise chart.

Detailed
Trek Graph
Day 1
Drive from Shimla to Kafnu
Altitude (Shimla): 2,200m / 7,200 ft
Altitude (Kafnu): 2,450m / 8,000 ft
Pick up: Old Shimla Bus Stand at 6:00 am
Drive Distance and Duration: 200 km | 7–8 hours
Altitude Gain: 800 ft
Cloakroom facilities are not available for the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek, as it starts in Kafnu and ends in Kaza. Please carry only essential clothing.
Day 2
Trek from Kafnu to Mulling
Altitude (Kafnu): 2,450m / 8,000 ft
Altitude (Mulling): 3,200 m / 10,700 ft
Trek Distance and Duration: 11 km | 7–8 hrs
Altitude Gain: 2,700 ft
Gradient: Moderate to Difficult
Landscape: Forest section (silver birch, pine, and oak trees)
Water Sources: Mountain rivers (glacial streams)
Packed lunch for the day
Day 3
Trek from Mulling to Kara
Altitude (Mulling): 3,200 m / 10,700 ft
Altitude (Kara): 3,600 m / 11,800 ft
Trek Distance and Duration: 6 km | 4–5 hrs
Altitude Gain: 1,100 ft
Gradient: Easy to Moderate
Landscape: Rocky terrain. The last 1 km is a steep ascent
Water Sources: Waterfall and streams
Day 4
Rest and Acclimatization at Kara
Altitude (Kara): 3,600 m / 11,800 ft
Kara Lake visit
Short walks around the campsite
Day 5
Trek from Kara to Phutsirang
Altitude (Kara): 3,600 m / 11,800 ft
Altitude (Phutsirang): 4,200 m / 13,700 ft
Trek Distance and Duration: 5 km | 4–5 hrs
Altitude Gain: 1,900 ft
Gradient: Moderate with continuous ascent
Landscape: A combination of stones and loose scree
Water Sources: Mountain stream
Day 6
Trek from Phutsirang to Bwaldar via Bhaba Pass
Altitude (Phutsirang): 4,200 m / 13,700 ft
Altitude (Bhaba Pass): 4,900 m / 16,100 ft
Altitude (Bwaldar): 4,080 m / 13,400 ft
Trek Distance and Duration: 17 km | 11–12 hrs
Altitude Gain of 2,400 ft, and then a loss of 2,700 ft
Gradient: Difficult – Continuous steep ascent till pass
Landscape: The scenery changes from lush green to cold desert
Day 7
Trek from Bwaldar to Mudh and Drive to Kaza
Altitude (Bwaldar): 4,080 m / 13,400 ft
Altitude (Mudh): 3,800 m / 12,500 ft
Altitude (Kaza): 3,700 m / 12,200 ft
Trek Distance and Duration: 12 km | 5–6 hrs
Drive Distance and Duration: 50 km | 2–3 hrs
Altitude Loss: 300 ft
Gradient: Easy to Moderate
Landscape: The region is dominated by red soil and small-sized red boulders
Day 8
Drive from Kaza to Manali
Altitude (Kaza): 3,700 m / 12,200 ft
Altitude (Manali): 2,050 m / 6,700 ft
Drive Distance and Duration: 200 km | 9–10 hrs
Altitude Loss: 5,500 ft
Buffer Day (Mandatory)
To ensure a safe and hassle-free trekking experience, all participants are required to keep one additional buffer day after the scheduled trek. This reserve day may be used in case of adverse weather, delayed pass crossings, or other unforeseen circumstances. If the buffer day is utilized, an additional charge of ₹2,500 + 5% GST per person will be applicable and must be paid directly to the Trek Leader.

How to Reach the Pin Bhaba Pass Trek
Pickup Information

To reach the Pin Bhaba Pass, it is necessary for everyone to travel to Shimla – the main pickup point of the trek. The meeting place is “Shimla Old Bus Stand.” Once you have reached Shimla, TTH will manage the rest of your travel arrangements only if you have opted for TTH's pick-up service. You can select this option during the booking process by adding it as an add-on (book it at least 10 days before the trek date). To avoid any kind of delays, it is advisable to reach Shimla one day earlier.

Please Note: The most ideal and practical route to reach Shimla is from Chandigarh.

Options to Reach Shimla
Arrival by Bus:
Shimla is very well connected by highways. HRTC buses run frequently from Chandigarh to Shimla (120 km), taking approximately 3.5 to 4.5 hours depending on traffic and the type of bus. The HRTC buses usually depart from ISBT Sector 43 in Chandigarh, which is the primary boarding hub for both Government and Private buses. You can catch AC or Non-AC buses early in the morning, in the afternoon, in the evening, and at night.
According to the HRTC schedule, the first bus from Chandigarh to Shimla departs at 12:00 AM and the last at 08:20 PM. We highly recommend that you visit the HRTC website to check the bus schedule according to your travel plan.
From ISBT (Kashmiri Gate), Delhi, non-AC ordinary HRTC buses run frequently to Shimla. They depart from 05:00 AM in the morning and 11:40 PM at night. The HRTC Volvo buses depart from 12:00 AM to 11:00 PM. The distance is about 340 to 360 km, which takes around 7–10 hours depending on traffic and weather.
Note: Based on our and trekkers’ prior experience, we highly recommend you choose government buses over private buses. Government buses are generally more reliable, while private buses are more prone to delays.

Arrival by Air:
The direct flight to Shimla is only the “Alliance Air,” which usually operates from Delhi. The nearest airport to Shimla is at Jubbarhatti, which is around 22 km from the city. It’s important to note that the airstrip is small, plus the conditions here are overwhelmed by mountainous winds. Thus, flights are subject to last minute cancellations or diversions to Chandigarh.

Location	Travelling Guide/Travel Instruction
DELHI	
The Indira Gandhi International Airport, Delhi, is the prime airport that offers a direct flight to Shimla Airport (SLV) in Jubbarhatti.
As for the airlines, the major airline that offers a direct flight to Shimla is the“Alliance Air”.
From Delhi Airport, you can also opt to travel by bus as mentioned above.
CHANDIGARH	
Since Chandigarh is a better option and it takes less time from here then you can book a connecting flight to Chandigarh via Delhi/Hyd/Pune/Mumbai and then book a cab or take a bus to reach Shimla.
The distance between the Chandigarh Airport and the Chandigarh Bus Stand is 14 km.
After arriving at the Jubbarhatti Airport, you can book a cab or hire a taxi to Shimla, which is approximately 22 km away.

Note— Flights from Delhi to Chandigarh are often quite expensive, and even after reaching Chandigarh, you still need to continue by road to Manali. So, if you are travelling from outside Delhi and considering a flight to Chandigarh, you may want to opt for a more economical alternative instead. If you have sufficient time, travelling by road from Delhi to Manali is both comfortable and scenic. You can take an overnight or morning bus from ISBT Delhi, or hire a private cab directly to Manali.

Note: The flights to Jubbarhatti are highly dependent on the weather conditions and seasonality. So, we firmly advise you to arrive a day or two before your trek. Also, due to the same reason, most travelers instead fly to Chandigarh or Delhi and then continue by road.

Arrival By Train
Shimla doesn’t have a standard broad-gauge railway station and tracks that can connect to the mainland railway stations. However, major regular trains go to Kalka, which is the classic railway route to Shimla. Moreover, Kalka railway station is well-connected to Delhi, Chandigarh, Kolkata, and Mumbai.
After reaching Kalka, you can board a bus or hire a taxi to Shimla.

Another nearest railway station to Shimla is Chandigarh Railway Station. From there, you can travel to ISBT Sector 43 (approximately 10–11 km), and then you can take an overnight bus to Manali.

How to Reach Kafnu
The road journey from Shimla to Kafnu is about 200 km and normally takes 7–10 hours, depending on the weather and road conditions. You can reach Kafnu village by any direct HRTC bus. These buses depart from Shimla ISBT or Old Bus Stand.

The route is: Shimla → Narkanda → Rampur → Jeori → Wangtoo → Kafnu

This mode is best for budget travelers, solo trekkers, backpackers, etc.

Another way is by shared taxi or shared Jeep. This is the most common option for trekkers heading to Pin Bhaba Pass.

Drop-Off Information
The designated drop-off point is the Manali Old Gov bus stand around 6:30 pm to 7:30 pm .
Please consider planning your subsequent travel arrangements after 8:00 pm.
It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Manali.
TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Why is Hampta Pass a Must- Do Trek?
Hampta Pass is a high-altitude crossover trek that connects the lush green Kullu Valley to the stark, arid landscapes of the Lahaul region.
The dramatic shift in scenery during this trek is nothing short of stunning, with every day bringing a new backdrop.
Stream crossings add a thrill to the adventure, especially as you wade through icy, gushing waters using ropes for support.
Standing at over 14,000 ft, the view from Hampta Pass showcases towering peaks and dramatic valleys.
The close-up views of Mt. Indrasan and Mt. Deo Tibba are a visual treat for mountain lovers and photography enthusiasts alike.
Each campsite like Shea Goru, Chhika, and Balu Ka Ghera, is scenically placed, surrounded by mountains and streams.
The trek offers a moderate challenge, making it ideal for beginners with good preparation who want to experience a high-altitude pass without extreme difficulty.
A major highlight of Hampta Pass trek is the visit to the stunning Chandratal Lake, a crescent-shaped alpine lake with unreal blue hues.
During the summer and monsoon months, the valley blooms with vibrant wildflowers and diverse wildlife.
Major Attractions of the Hampta Pass Trek
Hampta Pass: The most significant part of the trek, the Hampta Pass crossing is an adventure in itself. Being at a high altitude it tests your endurance and strength, but the surrounding views make up for it. The pass itself is a wide passage that goes on for some time, once you are at the pass you get closest to the Deotibba and Indrasan peak.
River Crossings: The trek is full of exciting moments, and crossing rivers with the help of ropes is one of the most adventurous parts. You’ll cross fast-flowing mountain streams and icy rivers at two points, once while trekking from Chika to Balu Ka Ghera, and again from Shea Goru to Chatru. These rope crossings not only give a thrilling experience but also bring out teamwork and courage, making them some of the most memorable highlights of the trek.
Scenic campsites: Another beautiful aspect of the Hampta Pass Trekking experience is the chance to camp at some truly magical spots. From the open green meadows of Chika, where you fall asleep to the sound of a flowing river, to Balu ka Gera, surrounded by snow-draped peaks, and the remote setting of Shea Goru, each campsite feels like a beautiful resting spot.
Chandratal: An additional beauty to the trek, Chandratal Lake is a serene glacial lake located in the Spiti region. The name means “Moon Lake,” and true to its name, it has a quiet, otherworldly charm. Surrounded by mountains and set against the wide blue sky, the lake’s crystal-clear waters reflect the changing colors of the sky throughout the day. Reaching Chandratal at the end of the trek feels like a dream, and spending time here is peaceful and calming.
Mountain Views: The Hampta Pass trek offers some of the best panoramic views of the Himalayas. From snow-covered peaks to deep valleys and glacier fields, the scenery constantly evolves. On clear days, you can spot peaks like Indrasan, Deo Tibba, and their glaciers. These views not only offer great photo opportunities but also a deep sense of connection to the majestic scale of the mountains around you.
The change in Terrains: Hampta Pass Trek takes you through a variety of terrains. The journey begins in dense forests,moves into wide green meadows, and gradually leads to rocky trails, snowy slopes, and barren deserts of Spiti. This continuous change keeps the trek exciting and refreshing. It’s like walking through different worlds within a few days.
Hampta Pass Trek – A Journey Through Two Worlds
The Hampta Pass trek is one of the most dramatic and equally breathtaking treks in India. It is also called a “crossover path” because it connects two entirely different Himalayan landscapes in a single trip. It offers a visually captivating transition from lush green Kullu Valley into the barren, moon-like landscapes of Lahaul Valley. One moment you are walking through dense forests, mesmerizing waterfalls, and flower-laden meadows, and the next you are surrounded by rocky mountains and cold desert terrain.

Hampta Pass is the perfect balance of adventure, beauty, and accessibility for beginners as well as experienced trekkers. This striking contrast makes it truly special, which is why the route remains visually rich throughout the journey. The biggest attraction of the trek is crossing the Pass itself. Standing at the top provides you with incredible views of snow-covered Himalayan peaks and deep valleys. The sense of accomplishment at the pass is unforgettable.

This trek is also famous for its scenic campsites, which are surrounded by steep mountains, glacial rivers, and open meadows. You have to cross the freezing waters of different streams a couple of times, which adds an exciting adventure to the journey. Finally, the excursion to the Chandratal Lake is the icing on the cake. This lake is famous for its crystal-clear blue water and stunning mountain reflections.

What Can You Expect During the Hampta Pass Trek?
The uniqueness of the Hampta Pass trek makes it stand out from the majority of treks in India.

One of the most distinct features you can expect on this journey is the dramatic change in landscapes. In the first half of the trip, you are enjoying the lush green landscapes of the Kullu Valley, and as you cross the pass, the scenery suddenly transforms into the barren and rugged terrain of the Lahaul Valley.
The next unique feature you should expect on this trek is the adventurous river crossings. There are moments when you have to walk through icy glacier streams that flow rapidly from the mountains. These crossings add an element of excitement and fun to make the journey more immersive. Snow is another key highlight, and you can find a few snow patches near the pass, making the journey thrilling and visually stunning.
The next great feature you can expect here is the exceptionally beautiful campsites. Chika feels like a peaceful Himalayan paradise, which is surrounded by vast green meadows, wildflowers, and snow-covered mountains. Balu Ka Ghera is located in a wide valley dominated by towering rocky peaks. The terrain here has broad stretches of riverbed shaped by glaciers and streams. Shea Goru is surrounded by snowfields, glaciers, and rugged mountains, transforming it into a cold desert-like beauty.
The next thing you can expect on this trek is constantly changing weather conditions. Bright sunshine can suddenly turn into mist, rain, or snowfall, adding unpredictability to the adventure. Overall, the Hampta Pass trek combines adventure, natural beauty, contrasting landscapes, and high-altitude excitement.
Hanging glaciers are the next prominent highlight to expect on this trek, especially in the months of June and July. You can spot them near “Balu Ka Ghera” and “Shea Goru” campsites, suspended dramatically along the mountain slopes.
How to Get Fit for the Hampta Pass Trek
Before going on a moderate trek such as the Hampta Pass, it’s crucial to follow a solid fitness regimen for ideally 3 months beforehand. Your workout should be a balanced mix of cardio and strength.

Focus on improving cardiovascular endurance through activities like brisk walking, jogging, cycling, or stair climbing. For strength training, you should do exercises such as squats, lunges, planks, and push-ups. This will help you build stamina and stability.

You can also practice hiking with a loaded backpack to prepare your body for real trekking conditions. Finally, flexibility and balance exercises, including yoga and stretching, reduce the risk of injuries and muscle cramps.

For more information regarding fitness preparation, click this link and download our trek exercise chart.

Best Time to Do the Hampta Pass Trek
The best time for the Hampta Pass Trek is during the monsoon months from June to September. The monsoon season transforms this entire region into a lush and vibrant paradise. These months offer pleasant weather, blooming meadows, flowing rivers, clear skies, and just the right amount of snow in higher regions.

If you want to experience snow on the trail, June is the ideal month to plan your trek. Early June usually offers snow-covered sections.

July and August are considered the peak months when trails are accessible, and nature is at its finest. The scenery is especially stunning, with waterfalls, wildflowers, and rich greenery throughout the route.

September is another excellent month for the trek, which brings crisp mountain views, clear skies, and beautiful autumn colours.

Overall, late June to early September is considered the best time for the Hampta Pass trek, offering the perfect balance of adventure, scenic beauty, and favourable trekking conditions.

Mid-June to Early July
This season is best for snow experience + adventure. You will see: snowfields near the pass, frozen streams and icy crossings, stark contrast between valleys and mountains.The daytime temperature ranges from 10 to 20 degrees; nighttime temperature drops to 0 to 4 degrees.

July to August
This season is best for green landscapes + waterfalls. You’ll see: bright green meadows, wildflowers, rapidly flowing rivers, and misty mountain views. The daytime temperature in July remains 10 to 18 degrees; nighttime temperature: -2 to 6 degrees. In August, the average daytime temperature is 13 to 20 degrees, and at night, it drops to 0 degrees or slightly below.

Post-Monsoon Autumn (September)
This season has great overall trekking conditions. Many experienced trekkers consider this season a sweet spot. You’ll see: crustal-clear skies, refreshing greenery, stable weather, clear mountain views, and safer river crossings.

October
This season is best for quiet trails and a cold mountain atmosphere. This is when the trekking season starts ending. You’ll see: dry landscapes with golden foliage, crisp mountain air, colder nights, and peaceful campsites. In October, the average daytime temperature ranges from 12 to 20 degrees, while nights drop to 0 to -6 degrees.

How Difficult Is the Hampta Pass Trek?
Hampta Pass is considered a moderate trek that spans roughly 24 km over a period of 5 days. It’s a beginner-friendly trek, but still requires good physical fitness due to certain challenges such as steep climbs, rocky terrain, tricky river crossings, and unpredictable weather conditions.

Here are the factors that come into play while doing this trek:

Quick Altitude Gain: After crossing the Manali/Jobra region, the trek gains altitude rapidly, touching 14,100 feet within just a few days. This quick increase in the ascent also increases the risk of altitude sickness.
The Summit Day: The pass-crossing day is particularly strenuous, which involves a steep climb and long hours of walking. The descent toward the Lahaul side demands careful footing most of the time.
The route also features river crossings, rocky scree sections, and narrow overhangs.
How Did the Hampta Pass Trek Get Its Name?
Historically, Hampta Pass derives its name from a nearby village of Hampta (or Hampta) that lies in the Kullu region. The mountain pass took on the name “Hampta Pass”, and the trekking route was later named after the pass itself.

According to the Hindu mythology, it is believed that the name of the village was derived from a sage (rishi) named Hampta, who meditated there. It has the Mahabharata connection as well. The Pandavas took this route (Hampta Pass to Chandratal) on their quest to heaven.

The geographic origin of this place explains that the pass had been widely used by the local Gaddi shepherds. These people used to move their sheep and cattle between the lush green meadows of Kullu and the rugged semi-desert landscapes of Lahaul and Spiti. The Hampta village is located just below this old route, which is why the name stuck.

How to Reach Manali
Pickup Information

If you choose TTH’s pickup service, TTH will manage the rest of your travel arrangements once you have reached the assembly point of the trek in Manali. You can select this option during the booking process by adding it as an add-on.

To reach Hampta Pass, you have to first travel to Manali, which is the main base camp. Before that, you can arrive at Chandigarh, Delhi, Pathankot, or Bhuntar airport.

Please Note: The most ideal place to reach Manali is from Chandigarh.

Arrival by Bus:
If you are taking a bus for Manali, then try to take the bus from Chandigarh, as it’s more convenient.

To take the bus from Chandigarh, first reach the Inter-State Bus Terminus (ISBT) in Sector 43. From there you can take a direct overnight bus to Manali, which will take approx. 6 - 10 hours to reach.
According to the HRTC schedule, the first bus for Manali departs at 3:40 AM and the last one runs until 9:00 PM. Although, we highly recommend that you visit the HRTC website to check the bus schedule according to your travel plan.
From Delhi, reach the ISBT Kashmiri Gate and take a direct bus to Manali, which takes around 14 hours to reach. A pro tip: numerous buses leave for Manali throughout the day, but departures peak between 6:00 PM and 11:55 PM.
(We highly recommend choosing government buses over private ones; based on our experience, private buses are more prone to delays.) Government buses are generally more reliable. Whichever option you choose, ensure you reach Manali by 10:00 AM on the pickup day.

Arrival by Air:
For all the international trekkers opting for flight, it is the most preferred option to reach Bhuntar Airport (Kullu-Manali Airport).

Location	Travelling Guide/Travel Instruction
DELHI	
The Indira Gandhi International Airport, Delhi, is the prime airport that offers a direct flight to Bhuntar Airport.
As for the airlines, the major airline that offers a direct flight to Manali is the“Alliance Air”. Other than that Air India, IndiGo, and others also operate on this route.
From Delhi Airport, you can also opt to travel by bus as mentioned above.
CHANDIGARH	
Since Chandigarh is a better option and it takes less time from here then you can book a connecting flight to Chandigarh via Delhi/Hyd/Pune/Mumbai and then book a cab or take a bus to reach Manali.
The distance between the Chandigarh Airport and Chandigarh Bus Stand is 14 km.
After arriving at the Bhuntar Airport (Kullu-Manali Airport), you can either take a bus or book a cab to reach the pickup point (Woodbine Hotel).

Note— The flights to Bhuntar are highly dependent on the weather conditions and seasonality. So, we firmly advise you to arrive a day or two before your trek.

Arrival By Train
There is no railway station in Manali, so there’s no direct train that goes there. The nearest major railway stations are: Joginder Nagar (145 – 160 km), Chandigarh (260 - 270 km), Ambala Cantt (320 – 330 km), and Pathankot (390 – 400 km).

From a connectivity point of view, Chandigarh railway station is ideal for reaching Manali. To know more about the train schedule, you can visit the IRCTC website. From Chandigarh Railway Station you need to travel to ISBT Sector 43 (approximately 10–11 km), and then you can take an overnight bus to Manali.

Note: If you prefer to travel on your own, you can choose between a government bus or a private cab. Your trek coordinator will assist you with all the necessary details and guidance for arranging the bus or cab booking.

Drop-Off Information
The designated drop-off point is the Woodbine Hotel in Manali. around 6:30 pm to 7:30 pm.
Please consider planning your subsequent travel arrangements after 8:00 pm.
It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Manali.
TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Why is Bhrigu Lake a Must-Do Trek?
The Bhrigu Lake trek is widely regarded as one of the best short Himalayan treks in India, completed in just 4 days.
The first thing that surprises you is the landscape. Its meadows are often compared to the famous grasslands of Europe, but what makes them truly special is their untouched Himalayan charm.
Located at an altitude of 13,700 feet, Lake Bhrigu appears deep blue, emerald-green, or partially frozen depending on the season.
According to the Hindu mythology, the lake holds deep spiritual significance as one of the revered sages, Maharishi Bhrigu, meditated here.
One of the defining features of this trek is its stunning campsites, offering a surreal natural beauty during the day and a magical experience of sleeping under a starlit sky.
You will enjoy the breathtaking bird’s-eye views of Manali and Solang Valley. The trek also offers an elevated vantage point, allowing you to admire stunning landscapes and majestic mountain ranges.
As you travel along the route, you'll be treated to sweeping panoramic views of the magnificent Pir Panjal and Dhauladhar mountain ranges.
As you navigate the trek, you step on the endless stretches of green grass spread across the mountains like nature's own carpet.
Wildflowers are like icing on the cake – they appear to bloom in every direction. These low-growing perennial flowers look incredibly beautiful against the stunning backdrop of mighty peaks.
In this journey, trekkers often go through an experience that is difficult to put into words—but impossible to forget.
This is the perfect trek for beginners due to its accessibility. Where many high-altitude treks require proper preparation, Bhrigu Lake offers spectacular beauty without demanding extreme technical skills.
At sunrise and sunset, the sky above Bhrigu Lake becomes a breathtaking spectacle. The golden orange glow casts a stunning reflection on the pristine lake, which is mesmerizing to say the least.
Being a relatively short and beginner-friendly trek, the Bhrigu Lake journey is perfectly suitable for families and friends who are looking for a peaceful adventure.
Major Attractions of the Bhrigu Lake Trek
The Bhrigu Lake trek is one of those extraordinary journeys that combine adventure, peace, spirituality, and breathtaking scenery in one unforgettable experience. Here are the highlights that make it what it is:

Vast Alpine Meadows
One of the most captivating features of this trek is its expansive meadows. Unlike many Himalayan treks that wind through dense forests for much of the route, the Bhrigu Lake quickly rises above the tree line, revealing stunning grasslands and panoramic mountain landscapes. Due to their rolling, undulating terrain and extended stretch of greenery, these lush meadows are often compared to the well-known grasslands of Europe.

Bhrigu Lake
The obvious main highlight of the trek is the enchanting Bhrigu Lake, a high-altitude glacial lake nestled in the embrace of the Himalayas. According to legends, Sage Bhrigu meditated beside this lake to seek deep spiritual liberation and connect with higher cosmic powers. This sacredness makes the lake a revered destination for many. What makes it particularly special is its ever-changing appearance depending on the season and weather.

Rola Kholi Campsite
The Rola Kholi campsite is probably the most scenic and beautiful camping location on the trek. What makes it beautiful isn't just one feature—it's the way mountains, meadows, streams, and sky come together in a vast landscape. During the summer and the monsoon season, this place turns vibrant green and is wonderfully dotted with wildflowers. The grandeur and scale of the Himalayas surrounding this campsite exceed every stretch of imagination.

Seven Sisters Peaks Viewpoint
Many sections of the trail offer views of the famous Seven Sisters Peaks, a beautiful cluster of seven peaks emerging from a single mountain. These stunning peaks form an impressive backdrop to the trek and look absolutely striking during clear weather. The sight of these peaks rising above the meadows is definitely a photographer’s delight.

Sweeping Himalayan Vistas
Throughout the trek, you’ll be greeted with breathtaking panoramic views of several prominent Himalayan ranges. The route offers sweeping vistas of the Pir Panjal Range and Dhauladhar Range, along with numerous snow-clad peaks. Not only that, the changing colors of the mountains during sunrise and sunset create unforgettable visual spectacles. These panoramic visuals are among the most photographed segments of the trek.

Picturesque Gulaba Forests
One of the most remarkable aspects of the trek is the Gulaba forests, from where the journey typically begins. The initial section of the trek takes you through forests dominated by oak, cedar, pine, and deodar trees. Walking through this stretch is refreshing, especially during the morning hours when sunlight filters through the canopy. In the monsoon, this section is at its greenest, while the mist in the middle of the forest provides an otherworldly vibe.

Sunrise and Sunset Views
The sunrise and sunset views on this trek are among its most captivating highlights. At sunrise, the first rays of the sun illuminate the snow-capped peaks, painting the landscape in shades of gold, orange, and pink. Sunset is no less spectacular, as the fading sunlight creates a truly magical scene. The changing colors of the sky, combined with panoramic views of peaks such as Hanuman Tibba, Deo Tibba, and the Pir Panjal range, make for an unforgettable experience.

Alluring Views of Manali and Sonalg Valley
From multiple elevated sections along the trail, the Bhrigu Lake trek rewards you with breathtaking panoramic views of Manali and Solang Valley. These stunning vantage points offer a unique bird’s-eye perspective of the landscape below, revealing winding roads, dense forests, flowing rivers, and picturesque villages in the lap of majestic Himalayan mountains. The striking contrast between the vibrant valleys and the serene high-altitude surroundings makes these particular viewpoints one of the most memorable highlights of the trek.

Bhrigu Lake Trek: An Extraordinary Journey that Stays with You Forever
Some treks challenge you, but there are some that change you. The Bhrigu Lake trek belongs to the latter category, one that quietly enters your heart and leaves you utterly mesmerised from within. Located at an altitude of 13,700 feet in the picturesque Kullu Valley, the lake attracts trekkers, nature lovers, photographers, pilgrims, and spiritual seekers from across the world.

One of the most incredible attributes of this trek is the dramatic shift in the landscapes. The journey begins from the quaint village of Gulaba. In virtually no time after you start the trek, you are in the middle of a dense forest of oak, cedar, and pine before emerging into vast alpine meadows. When you look back and watch the distant Himalayan peaks lining the horizon, the view invariably creates a sense of wonder.

The rolling meadows of the Bhrigu Lake trek are among the most widely known, which are stretched endlessly along the horizon. What makes these grasslands particularly unique is the way they change as you gain altitude. From dense and bright to soft and lush, the transition is fascinating. Moreover, these meadows are incomplete without a striking display of wildflowers throughout different seasons. They transform every few weeks, unveiling new colours and textures as the season progresses.

The stretch of this trek you will remember the most is the ridge walk, where you just feel you are walking through the sky. As the valley drops away on either side of the trail, you are awestruck by the humble vastness of the landscape. The final ascent to the Bhrigu Lake is the most dramatic and scenic section, as it takes you to the ridge. From here, the Pir Panjal and Dhauladhar ranges stand firmly to stun you. Finally, the spiritual connection of the trek adds a profound dimension to the experience, uplifting it beyond an outdoor adventure into a meaningful journey through culture, heritage, and history.

Best Time to Visit the Bhrigu Lake Trek
The best time to do the Bhrigu Lake trek is from mid-June to early October. This time period offers the most memorable experience. Also, unlike many other Himalayan treks, Bhrigu Lake transitions dramatically from month to month due to its high altitude and easy access from Manali.

Here is the month-by-month breakdown:

June
This is the time when the snow meets summer, and that’s why it becomes the most magical transition period.
What you’ll see: snow patches + fresh green grass; streams overflowing with snowmelt.

Apart from that, you’ll experience comfortable temperatures, which is good for first-time trekkers.

July
In the Bhrigu Lake trek, you can define July as the green carpet month. The famous alpine meadows of this trek burst into bright green landscapes. Many trekkers tend to compare these rolling grasslands with those of Europe.
What you’ll see: Endless green ridges + rich mountain vegetation.

The stunning scenery and pleasant temperatures offer drone and photography opportunities.

August
This is the season of bloom as the meadows are dotted by numerous types of wildflowers. The stretches of Rola Kholi and Gulaba meadows turn into a giant natural flower garden.
What you’ll see: yellow and purple flowers; the most colorful month.

September
During this time, the skies become crystal clear, while the peaks look incredibly beautiful. It is generally considered as the best all-around time for trekkers.
What you’ll see: Long-range views of Pir Panjal range + excellent visibility.

The clearest mountain views and stable weather create the best photography conditions.


How Difficult is the Bhrigu Lake Trek?
The Bhrigu Lake trek is generally classified as easy to moderate. However, it can still pose challenges for first-time trekkers. The trek is not technical, but the combination of altitude, terrain, and unpredictable weather conditions demands preparation. It covers an approximate distance of 24 km, which takes around 4 days to complete.

Here are the challenges you might face on this trek:

Rapid Altitude Gain: This is probably the biggest challenge. In just a matter of days, you ascend from 2,000 meters to 4,200 meters. It’s important to note that people who are very fit might struggle because altitude affects everyone differently.
Thin Air: At altitudes above 4,000 meters, the air thins out. The oxygen levels near the lake become noticeably reduced. As a result, simple activities become harder to perform.
Sudden Weather Changes: One of the attributes of the Himalayan treks is the unpredictability of the weather. It can quickly change around Manali and the high meadows. In a single day, you can witness sunshine, strong winds, rain, or snow.
Rocky and Uneven Terrain: The trek features considerable stretches of rocky and uneven terrain. Just before the lake or summit push, the path leaves the grassy landscape behind, and you step onto the steep and zig-zag climbs.
History Behind the Bhrigu Lake Trek?
The Bhrigu Lake is named after Maharishi Bhrigu, one of the Saptarishis (Seven Great Sages). According to local legends, Maharishi Bhrigu is believed to have meditated near this lake, so the name stuck. People belonging to this place believe that the Sage Bhrigu blessed the waters of this lake himself, making it spiritually sacred.

Who exactly was Sage Bhrigu?
In the Hindu mythology, Maharishi Bhrigu holds a very prominent and pious position. One of the seven great sages (Saptarishis), Bhrigu was a mind-born son of Lord Brahma (one of the Trinity). He is considered the supreme scholar of astrology and Vedic knowledge. He is celebrated as the first compiler of predictive astrology, the author of the monumental Bhrigu Samhita.

There is a deep belief among the local people about the lake – that it never completely freezes, even during the harshest of the Himalayan winters. The devotees attribute this belief to the blessings of Sage Bhrigu. However, this is not a scientifically proven fact, but it surely adds to the lake’s mystical repute.

How to Reach the Bhrigu Lake Trek?
Arrival by Bus
If you are taking a bus for Manali, then try to take the bus from Chandigarh, as it’s more convenient.

To take the bus from Chandigarh, first reach the Inter-State Bus Terminus (ISBT) in Sector 43. From there, you can take a direct overnight bus to Manali, which will take approx. 6 - 10 hours to reach.
According to the HRTC schedule, the first bus for Manali departs at 3:40 AM and the last one runs until 9:00 PM. Although, we highly recommend that you visit the HRTC website to check the bus schedule according to your travel plan.
From Delhi, reach the ISBT Kashmiri Gate and take a direct bus to Manali, which takes around 14 hours to reach. A pro tip: numerous buses leave for Manali throughout the day, but departures peak between 6:00 PM and 11:55 PM.
(We highly recommend choosing government buses over private ones; based on our experience, private buses are more prone to delays.)Government buses are generally more reliable. Whichever option you choose, ensure you reach Manali by 10:00 AM on the pickup day.

Arrival by Air
For all the international trekkers opting for flight, it is the most preferred option to reach Bhuntar Airport (Kullu-Manali Airport).

Location	Travelling Guide / Travel Instruction
DELHI	The Indira Gandhi International Airport, Delhi, is the prime airport that offers a direct flight to Bhuntar Airport.
As for the airlines, the major airline that offers a direct flight to Manali is the “Alliance Air”. Other than that Air India, IndiGo, and others also operate on this route.
From Delhi Airport, you can also opt to travel by bus as mentioned above.
CHANDIGARH	Since Chandigarh is a better option and it takes less time from here then you can book a connecting flight to Chandigarh via Delhi/Hyd/Pune/Mumbai and then book a cab or take a bus to reach Manali.
The distance between the Chandigarh Airport and Chandigarh Bus Stand is 14 km.
After arriving at the Bhuntar Airport (Kullu-Manali Airport), you can either take a bus or book a cab to reach the pickup point (Woodbine Hotel).

Note: The flights to Bhuntar are highly dependent on the weather conditions and seasonality. So, we firmly advise you to arrive a day or two before your trek.

Arrival by Train
There is no railway station in Manali, so there’s no direct train that goes there. The nearest major railway stations are Joginder Nagar (145 – 160 km), Chandigarh (260 – 270 km), Ambala Cantt (320 – 330 km), and Pathankot (390 – 400 km).

From a connectivity point of view, Chandigarh railway station is ideal for reaching Manali. To know more about the train schedule, you can visit the IRCTC website. From Chandigarh Railway Station you need to travel to ISBT Sector 43 (approximately 10–11 km), and then you can take an overnight bus to Manali.

Note: If you prefer to travel on your own, you can choose between a government bus or a private cab. Your trek coordinator will assist you with all the necessary details and guidance for arranging the bus or cab booking.

How to Reach Gulaba
Gulaba is the place from where the actual trek to the Bhrigu Lake starts. It is located around 21 km from Manali on the road towards Rohtang Pass. So, to reach Gulaba, you can avail different options –

Option 1: Shared Taxi: It is easily available during trekking season. It will take 40 to 50 minutes.
Option 2: Personal Taxi: A private taxi will be great if you’re travelling in a group. It provides flexibility in terms of time.
Option 3: Local Bus: It’s the cheapest option, but less frequent.

Pick Up Information
If you choose TTH’s pickup service, TTH will manage the rest of your travel arrangements once you have reached the assembly point of the trek in Manali. You can select this option during the booking process by adding it as an add-on.
To reach Hampta Pass, you have to first travel to Manali, which is the main base camp. Before that, you can arrive at Chandigarh, Delhi, Pathankot, or Bhuntar airport.
Please Note: The most ideal place to reach Manali is from Chandigarh.

Drop-Off Information
The designated drop-off point is the Woodbine Hotel in Manali around 6:30 pm to 7:30 pm.
Please consider planning your subsequent travel arrangements after 8:00 pm.
It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Manali.
TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Why is Rupin Pass a Must-Do trek ?
Rupin Pass Trek is one of the most scenic treks in Himachal, it takes you through diverse landscapes, changing from lush green valleys to snow-covered terrains.
Every turn during this trek will surprise you with some amazing sceneries, including cascading waterfalls, snow bridges, and panoramic mountain vistas.
Crossing the thrilling Rupin Pass is a memorable adventure in itself.
The waterfalls during this trek will leave you in awe with their beauty, especially the three stage Rupin waterfall, which looks magical.
You get a unique experience of trekking besides a waterfall, with the gushing water flowing besides you.
Witness the Jakha village, also known as the hanging village, because of its appealing position on the mountain side.
The trek passes through local villages, providing you an opportunity to engage with the local culture and witness the Kathkuni architecture of the houses.
The final push towards the pass involves walking on the snow and climbing over ridges, giving you a thrilling trekking experience.
Even the campsites of this trek are at scenic locations, including the Dhandreyash Thatch, where you camp amid vast meadows with a view of the Rupin waterfall.
Rupin Pass trek- A Journey through Meadows, Waterfalls, and Snowy Trails
Himachal is well known for its stunning natural beauty, and you can experience it at its best during the Rupin Pass trek. This trek is one of the most thrilling and picturesque adventures in the Himalayas, offering beautiful landscapes, diverse trails, and an immersive cultural experience. It takes you through dense forests, lush meadows, and rocky terrain before reaching the majestic Rupin Pass. The ever-changing scenery keeps the journey exciting, with every step revealing a new wonder of nature. Along the way, you will witness cascading waterfalls, cross wooden bridges over crystal-clear streams, and walk through high-altitude meadows decorated with little vibrant wildflowers.

The trek begins from Bawta village, a charming settlement known for its traditional Kath-Kuni style wooden houses, made from sturdy deodar cedar wood. These homes not only add to the village’s rustic charm but also provide a warm and cozy atmosphere, perfect for the chilly mountain nights. The trail takes you through dense oak and rhododendron forests, where the air is filled with the fresh scent of pine and the chirping of birds. The trek offers you picturesque panoramic views of snow-capped peaks and valleys stretching as far as the eye can see. One of the most memorable moments during the Rupin Pass trek is trekking alongside a magnificent waterfall, its roaring sound filling the air and the cool mist refreshing you as you pass by.

Major Attractions along the Rupin Pass Trek
Hanging Village of Jakha: Jakha is known as the “Hanging Village” due to its steeply built houses that appear stacked on top of one another. This small Himalayan settlement is the last inhabited village before entering the wild terrain of the trek.
Buransh Kandi: A short stretch of rhododendron forests adds a magical touch to the trail. If you trek during the blooming season, you'll witness rhododendrons in full bloom, painting the landscape in stunning shades of red and pink. Even the fallen petals enhance the beauty of the path. Though this section is brief, it creates a beautiful contrast against the lush green surroundings.
Dandreyash Thatch, a vast expanse: This vast open meadow, surrounded by towering peaks and a view of the Rupin waterfall, serves as one of the best camping spots. In the summer, it is dotted with wildflowers, making it look like something straight out of a dream.
Rupin Waterfall: The best part of the trek is the Rupin Waterfall. The river flows down from a great height in multiple tiers, and you even get a chance to camp near the base of the waterfall which surely is a remarkable experience, but the best part is you get to trek besides the waterfall.
Rupin Pass– The Final Push: Crossing the Rupin Pass at an impressive elevation of 4,700m (15,350 ft) is a rewarding experience. The narrow Rupin Gully, with its snow-covered path, adds an element of adventure. Once at the top, the view of the surrounding Himalayan peaks makes all the effort worthwhile.
Descent into Sangla Valley: Unlike most treks that retrace their steps, the Rupin Pass Trek ends in a different location. The final descent leads to the beautiful Sangla Valley in Himachal Pradesh, known for its apple orchards and the Baspa River flowing through it.
What you can Expect during Rupin Pass Trek?
You can expect your journey to Rupin Pass Trek, to be a memorable and a scenic adventure amid the beautiful Himalayas. The trails offer stunning views of cascading waterfalls, crystal- clear rivers, and scenic campsites too. Something that makes this trek a one of a kind is the rare experience of trekking alongside waterfalls, something not often found on other trails. The campsites during this trek are perfect spots for resting and acclimatizing along with stunning views, they provide you a peaceful escape too. Dandreyash Thatch is one such campsite that would make you instantly fall in love with the surroundings, and you would surely feel like staying there for even longer. Along with scenic beauty you can expect a rich cultural experience and friendly locals too.

The trek is moderate to difficult, with steep inclines and challenging sections that will test your endurance. The final stretch is covered in snow, making the last push even more exciting and adventurous. Along with the beautiful surroundings, you should also be aware of the possibility of AMS (Acute Mountain Sickness) as you gain altitude. To prevent this, drink plenty of water, maintain a slow and steady pace, take time to acclimatize, and listen to your trek leaders. Rest when needed, stay positive, and trust in yourself as you tackle this incredible journey.

Best Time to do Rupin Pass Trek
The best time to do the Rupin Pass trek is during the summer and autumn months, specifically in May, June, October, and November. During this period, the trails are at their best, they are easy to navigate and trek on a perfect blend of navigability and beauty. The weather is more favorable, making the trek more enjoyable, with wildflower blooms and lush green meadows that add vibrant colors to the landscape. One of the most picturesque spots along the trek is the Buransh Kandi stretch, where blooming Rhododendrons enhance the beauty of the surroundings, during this time, creating a truly magical experience. The pass itself is most accessible during these months, as the snow cover is relatively less, and the weather conditions are ideal for a safe journey. Trekking during this time ensures you get to experience the full splendor of the trek in its most stunning form.

Rupin Pass is the perfect choice if you're looking to experience the serenity, adventure, and natural beauty of trekking in Himachal. The trek is full of surprises, and you’ll never want it to end as every moment offers something new and exciting. If you’re ready for a challenge, choose Rupin Pass and let the stunning beauty of the Himalayas amaze you at every step.

How to Reach Shimla Himachal Pradesh
How to Reach Shimla Himachal Pradesh
It is essential for everyone to arrive at Shimla (05:30 am)
Meeting Place: Shimla Old Bus Stand.

Once you have reached Shimla, TTH will manage the rest of your travel arrangements, if you have opted for TTH's pick-up service, you can select this option during the booking process by adding it as an add-on (book it at least 10 days before trek date)

It is advisable to reach Shimla a day before to avoid any kind of delays.

Options to reach Shimla:

First, you can arrive at Chandigarh, Delhi, Kalka, or Shimla airport. The journey from these locations to Shimla is explained below.

1. Take overnight bus to Shimla from Delhi or Chandigarh, taking a bus from Chandigarh is the most ideal option.

-Chandigarh to Shimla Bus Stand : 120 km | 4 hr.

-Delhi to Shimla Bus Stand : 350 km | 10 hr.

( We strongly recommend going for the govt. Buses over the private ones outside the bus station as based on the experience we have found that there are very high chances of delay involved with private buses. Also, govt. Buses are always more reliable. Whichever bus you choose, just make sure to reach Shimla at least by 05:30 am. )

2. Take a Train to Kalka and drive to Shimla. One can take the pleasure of a toy train, If you're arriving by Train, then come one day in advance.

3. Take a flight to Shimla airport (Jubbarhatti airport) (22 km, 50 min). If you're arriving by air, then come one day in advance.

Drop-Off Information
The designated drop-off point is Shimla old bus stand.

Reach Shimla by 11:00 pm to 1:00 am, Please arrange your hotel and make further bookings in Shimla accordingly. 

It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Shimla.

( If you prefer to travel independently, you can either take a government bus or book a private cab. Your trek coordinator will provide guidance on how to arrange for the bus or cab booking. )

TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Why Buran Ghati Is A Must-Do Trek ?
Buran Ghati Trek combines the best elements of various Himalayan trails, offering unparalleled scenic beauty throughout the journey.
The trek takes you through forests of birch, maple, pine, and oak trees, providing a rich sensory experience.
The trek includes technical sections, such as crossing the challenging Buran Ghati Pass, which demands good maneuvering skills.
Visit the sacred Chandranahan Lake, believed to have emanated by the Gods, offering stunning alpine scenery.
Explore the beautiful Dayara Meadows, a scenic stretch with grazing horses and a backdrop of snow-covered mountains.
Pass through ancient villages, Janglik and Barua, showcasing Himalayan culture and architecture.
Encounter the challenging Buran Pass, which transforms into a wall of ice until June, providing a rappelling opportunity.
Experience the breathtaking Litham campsite, nestled in the confluence of two valleys with stunning waterfalls.
Enjoy panoramic views of the majestic Dhauladhar Range, one of the lower Himalayan mountain chains.
Witness remote peaks popping above each other, creating a spectacular visual treat.
Buran Ghati offers a comprehensive trekking adventure, encompassing cultural richness, thrilling challenges, and diverse landscapes.
Major Attractions of Buran Ghati Trek
Beautiful Dayara Meadows: This is one of the best parts of the trek and also the most scenic stretch. The Dayara Meadows come into view as you climb uphill from the village of Janglik and cross a dense coniferous. On one side of the meadow is a grassy stretch that climbs up the mountain and the other side simply drops down to the canyon below. On the meadows, you may observe the horses galloping or grazing the pastures, a beautiful sight to behold.
The Villages: Both villages Janglik and Barua are two major villages that we will pass on our trail. Janglik and Barua are both ancient Himalayan Villages that give you an understanding of the Himalayan culture. Pay close attention to the architecture, typical to the mountain villages. While crossing Barua, you will also see many fruit orchards as well.
Buran Pass And Snow wall: is a challenging trek that takes you up to an altitude of 15,000 ft. Until June, the pass itself becomes a wall of ice, giving you an incredible rappelling opportunity. It is because of this, that Buran Ghati is becoming more and more popular among trekkers.
Chandranahan Lake: Chandranahan Lake is yet another attraction of the trek. The mystical glacial lake is coveted by the locals. The lake remains frozen almost all year round and sitting here you can enjoy the serenity of the atmosphere and appreciate the visual treat
Litham Campsite: Litham campsite is one of the most scenic campsites along your route. It lies in the confluence of two valleys and you can simply sit here, getting mesmerized by the view. One of the most stunning views in the Litham campsite is the gorgeous waterfalls cascading down from the Chandranahan snout, offering an impressive sight.
Forest Cover: Along your trek, you will cross dense coniferous forest covers that have a charm of their own. Walking through birch, maple, pine and Oak trees as the birds chirp and the sounds of nature make their melody, your senses will be soothed completely.

If TTH cancels the Trip before arrival at the designated pick-up point due to unforeseen circumstances or Force Majeure, the Participant may choose from:
An alternate Trip/date.
A credit voucher valid for one (1) year.
Transfer to another Trip, with cost differences payable by the Participant.
If the Trip is abandoned post-arrival at the designated pick-up point, no cash refund or voucher shall be issued. The Trek Again Policy may apply at TTH’s discretion.
TTH shall not be liable for any associated travel costs such as flights, accommodation, or visa fees.
Force Majeure

Events beyond its control including but not limited to earthquakes, landslides, strikes, curfews, war, pandemic, government restrictions, heavy rainfall or snowfall, windstorms, road blockages, trail disruption, or withdrawal of permits, TTH shall not be held liable for any cancellation, delay, or service modification caused by Force Majeure.

Why is Friendship Peak Expedition a Must Do ?
The Friendship Peak Expedition or Friendship Peak Trek is an amazing journey through the beautiful landscapes of Himachal, taking you to an impressive height.
The summit offers an amazing visual treat of sky-touching Himalayan peaks including Deo Tibba, Indrasan, Shitidhar and Hanuman Tibba.
Watching the sunrise from the peak is an experience of a lifetime! The golden glow over the snow-capped peaks is a sight you will never forget.
You will experience the thrill of navigating through glaciers, steep slopes and rocky terrain using ice axes, crampons and ropes.
Every time you pause and look around, you will be amazed by the serene views of peaks and meadows.
Camping under a sky full of stars in the middle of the Himalayas and waking up to crisp mountain air and breathtaking views is an experience in itself.
Unlike other crowded treks, Friendship peak trek offers peace and solitude. The only sounds you will hear are the crunch of snow underfoot and the wind howling through the peak.
Trekking in tough conditions brings people closer, you will share challenges, laughter and support, creating lifelong memories.
Major Attractions of the Friendship Peak Trek
Mountain views: The mountain views during the trek are something extra- special. You get to see some of the highest peaks from the Pir Panjal and the Dhauladhar Ranges. Few of the prominent peaks visible include Deo Tibba, Indrasan and Hanuman Tibba.
Midnight Climb to the summit: A challenging yet fun part of the trek, is the midnight climb towards the summit. Gearing up in the middle of the night with your head torches on and climbing an icy trail in freezing temperatures, under a moonlit sky gives you the feeling of being a professional mountaineer.
Sunrise from the summit: If you begin trekking in midnight, you would reach the summit, by sunrise. The sunrise from the summit is another major highlight during your journey. The sunrise views from the top are sort of heavenly, you can watch the sky slowly changing colors and the snowy peaks glowing in the morning soft light. It would surely be an unforgettable memory.
Varied Landscapes: Throughout your trek, you get to experience varied Himalayan landscapes from verdant green meadows with Beas River flowing by, steep slopes, pine covers, moraine patches, boulders, and snow beds. The trek together gives you a beautiful experience of trekking in Himachal, with a mix of experiences in just a few kilometers.
Sightseeing Around Manali: The first day of the trek is scheduled for sightseeing in Manali. After arriving in Manali, you will spend the day visiting famous sightseeing spots like the Hadimba Temple and Vashist Kund. We recommend not visiting high-altitude locations like Rohtang Pass before the trek.

Quick
Detailed
Trek Graph
Day 1
Arrive in Manali
Altitude: 2000 m/ 6,550 ft.
Note: Only hotel stay is included in the cost (sightseeing and food are excluded).

Important note for Non-Indians: Foreign nationals will need a Foreigner Trek permit for the Friendship Peak Expedition. The charge for obtaining the Foreigner Trek permit is $100 per person. The payment will be made directly to the TTH representative upon arrival in Manali on day 1.

Day 2
Manali to Dhundi (Drive) to Lohali (Trek)
Altitude: Dhundi - 2850 m/ 9350 ft; Lohali - 3350 m/ 11,000 ft .
Drive Distance (Manali - Dhundi): 21 km | 1 .5 hrs.
Trek Distance (Dhundi - Lohali): 6 km | 3 to 4 hrs.
Day 3
Bakarthatch to Lady Leg
Altitude: 3,900 m/ 12,800 ft.
Trek Distance: 3 km | 3 hrs approx.
Day 4
Load ferry to Summit Camp and back to Lady Leg
Altitude(Summit Camp): 4,750 m/ 15,500 ft.
Trek Distance (One way): 2 km | 2-3hrs.
Day 5
Move to Summit Camp
Altitude(Summit Camp): 4,750 m/ 15,500 ft.
Trek Distance: 2 km | 2-3 hrs.
Day 6
Summit attempt and back to Lady Leg camp
Altitude(Friendship Peak): 5,287 m/17,346 ft.
Trek Distance(Summit camp to Friendship peak): 5 km one way | 8-9 hrs (Both way).
Trek Distance (Summit camp to Lady leg): 2 km | 1.5 hrs.
Day 7
Lady Leg to Dhundi to Manali
Trek Distance: 8 km, 4-5 hrs approx.
Drive Distance: 21 km, 1.5 hrs approx.
Note: Manali stay is not included in the cost

Day 8: Buffer Day

Buffer day will be used if the summit cannot be attempted due to any unforeseen circumstances.
Opting for buffer day is a team decision.
Recommended to keep a buffer day in your travel plan.
Buffer day cost is not included in the plan.
Buffer Day Charges: In the event that a buffer day is used, a charge of ₹50,000 per day will apply for the entire group. The total amount will be divided equally among the participants who utilize the buffer day.
An additional amount will be charged for buffer day on a per day, per person basis. 
If buffer day is not used in the expedition, use it for sightseeing around Manali.

Pick UP Information
Once you have reached the assembly point of the trek in Manali, TTH will manage the rest of your travel arrangements, if you have opted for TTH's pick-up service, you can select this option during the booking process by adding it as an add-on.
Options to Reach Manali
First, you can arrive at Chandigarh, Delhi, Pathankot, or Bhuntar airport. The journey from these locations to Manali is explained below.

1. Take an overnight bus to Manali from Delhi or Chandigarh, taking a bus from Chandigarh is the most ideal option.

Chandigarh to Manali Bus: 10 hours.
Delhi to Manali Bus: 14 hours is hectic and takes more time.
( We strongly recommend going for the govt. Buses over the private ones outside the bus station as based on the experience we have found that there are very high chances of delay involved with private buses. Also, govt. Buses are always more reliable. Whichever bus you choose, just make sure to reach Manali at 10 am. )

2. You can take a flight to Bhuntar Airport (but we would not recommend it because of the high prices of the flight and very high chances of cancellations), Manali is just a couple of hours away and you can easily get a bus or a private taxi till there. If you do plan to come via Bhuntar, make sure to reach at least a day before the trek starts.

3. You can even come via train, Chandigarh railway station is from where you can get direct buses to Kullu/Kasol/Manali that is 275 km from there and would take an overnight journey. Even in this case, reach a day before at Manali.

( If you prefer to travel independently, you can either take a government bus or book a private cab. Your trek coordinator will provide guidance on how to arrange for the bus or cab booking.)

Drop-Off Information
The designated drop-off point is the Woodbine Hotel in Manali around  6:30 pm to 7:30 pm .
Please consider planning your subsequent travel arrangements after 8:00 pm.
It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Manali.
TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Pin Parvati Pass Trek Overview
Trek Name:
Pin Parvati Pass Trek
Days:
11
Adventure:
Trekking
Base Camp:
Manali
Season:
Monsoon
Months:
July | August
Country:
India
Altitude:
17500 FT
Grade:
Challenging
Trail Type:
Cross over trail | Start in one valley, traverse the pass, and ends in another valley.
Food:
Meals while on trek & at Kalga guesthouse (Veg & Eggs)
Location:
Himachal Pradesh
Distance:
110 km
Rail Head:
Chandigarh is the nearest rail head to the base camp
Stay:
Camping (Twin sharing) & Hotel/Guesthouse (Separate for male & Female)
Airport:
Bhuntar, which is 52 km away from Manali
Why is Pin Parvati Pass a Must- Do Trek?
The landscapes during the Pin Parvati Pass trek change from lush greenery of Parvati valley to the dry, colorful mountains of Spiti.
During the trek you walk through forests, meadows, rocky paths, snowfields, and cold deserts all in one trek.
The natural hot water spring at Kheerganga is a perfect place to relax before the tough journey begins.
Camping in remote, scenic spots surrounded by high peaks and silence is an experience in itself.
You get to visit the source of the Parvati River, a quiet and powerful moment in the lap of nature.
The trek includes a few tricky sections where the trail isn’t always clear, but the trek leaders guide you through with care, turning challenges into an exciting part of the adventure.
Reaching the Pin Parvati Pass at 17,500 ft gives you amazing views of the two distinct valleys and a gushing feeling of success.
Trek through beautiful landscapes filled with wildflowers, and keep an eye out for Himalayan wildlife like blue sheep (bharal), marmots, and ibex along the way.
This trek challenges your body and mind, but the beauty, peace, and thrill make it truly worth it.
Major Attractions of the Pin Parvati Pass Trek
Kheerganga: Kheerganga is one of the most peaceful halts on the Pin Parvati trek. It is well-known for its natural hot water springs that feel comforting after a long day’s walk. Many trekkers also come here separately for a short and beginner-friendly trek. Along with the soothing water, Kheerganga holds spiritual significance for many, which adds a quiet charm to the place.
Source of Parvati River: In the initial part of the trek, you walk alongside the Parvati river, listening to its loud, flowing rhythm. As you progress further into the trail, you eventually reach the source of this gushing river. Watching the river emerge from the high mountain slopes is a powerful and humbling experience.
Challenging Sections: The Pin Parvati trek is more about its challenges, it demands focus and physical strength. Throughout the journey, you’ll face tricky stretches where the trail disappears into fields of boulders, narrow paths run beside deep gorges, and river crossings have to be done carefully, sometimes stepping over stones or holding onto ropes. These challenges may slow you down, but they also make the trek exciting and memorable.
Pin Parvati Pass: Pin Parvati Pass is the highest and most awaited point of the trek. It marks the connection between the green Parvati Valley and the dry, cold Pin Valley. Reaching here is no small task, it comes after days of effort and patience. The wide, open view from the pass is not only beautiful but also makes you feel proud to have made it to the top.
Changing Landscapes: One of the most fascinating aspects of this trek is how beautifully the scenery changes around you. The trail begins in lush forests and green meadows where you walk under trees and beside streams. As you gain altitude, the greenery gives way to rocky terrain, snow patches, and a frozen high pass. Finally, you descend into the stark, dry landscape of Spiti, a true shift in nature that keeps the journey interesting from start to end.
Cultural Diversity: The Pin Parvati trail also gives you a chance to experience different cultures along the way. At the beginning of the trek in Kalga and nearby villages, you come across traditional Himachali homes, wooden architecture, local fruits, and home-cooked meals. On the trail, you pass by shepherds who live with their flocks in the highlands. And once you cross into Spiti and reach the village of Mudh, you are welcomed by the warm and kind Bhotiya and Tibetan Buddhist locals.

If the trekker prefers cycling he/she should be able to cycle 25 km of distance in 65 minutes.

The designated drop-off point is the Woodbine Hotel in Manali around  6:30 pm to 7:30 pm .
Please consider planning your subsequent travel arrangements after 8:00 pm.
It's highly advisable to keep a buffer day in your travel plan. If the buffer day is not needed, it can be used to explore Manali.
TTH offers comfortable transportation through Tempo Traveler, Bolero, or equivalent vehicles. If you wish to upgrade your mode of transportation, please contact your trek coordinator for further assistance.

Kanamo Peak Trek Expedition Overview
Trek Name:
Kanamo Peak Trek Expedition
Days:
8
Adventure:
Trekking

Manali
Season:
Monsoon
